Section 1.1: Multiple Representations of Relationships

Learning target: Students will connect tables, graphs, equations, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

WTC

What's to Come

U1-S11-WTC-001 · WTC · Section 1.1 · Family S11-WTC-MULTIREP · WTC · Graph + Table + Equation + Context · constructed response

The graph shows a distance-time relationship for one trip.

  1. Record two ordered pairs you can read exactly from the graph.
  2. Describe how distance changes for each 1 unit of time.
  3. Make a table for time values 0, 2, 4, and 6.
  4. Write an equation that could describe the same relationship.
  5. Explain one way the graph, table, and equation show the same relationship.
Answer / solution

Possible exact points include $(0,3)$ and $(6,15)$. The distance increases 2 units per unit of time. A table is $(0,3),(2,7),(4,11),(6,15)$. One equation is $d=2t+3$. Each representation has the same starting value 3 and constant rate 2.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Students will connect tables, graphs, equations, and contexts that represent the same relationship.

Evidence/job: Preview equivalence across representations without requiring formal vocabulary.

Representation: Graph + Table + Equation + Context

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01 · DOK 2-3 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Example 1

U1-S11-EX-001 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-TABLE-TO-EQ · EX · Table + Equation · constructed response
xy
-2-5
-1-2
01
14

Write an equation for the relationship in the table and state the constant change in output for a 1-unit increase in input.

Answer / solution

The output increases by 3 each time, and $y=1$ when $x=0$, so $y=3x+1$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a table, graph, equation, or context from another representation of the same relationship.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Example 2

U1-S11-EX-002 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-EQ-TO-TABLE · EX · Equation + Table · constructed response

Use $y=-2x+5$ to complete a table for $x=-1,0,2,4$. Then name one ordered pair from your table.

Answer / solution

The outputs are 7, 5, 1, and -3. One ordered pair is $(2,1)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use features in one representation to work with a related table, graph, equation, or context.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Representation: Equation + Table

Example 3

U1-S11-EX-003 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-GRAPH-TO-EQ · EX · Graph + Equation · constructed response

Use the graph to write an equation and give two points that support your equation.

Answer / solution

The line has y-intercept 4 and slope -1, so $y=-x+4$. For example, $(0,4)$ and $(4,0)$ support the equation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use features in one representation to work with a related table, graph, equation, or context.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Representation: Graph + Equation

Example 4

U1-S11-EX-004 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-CONTEXT-TO-REP · EX · Context + Equation + Table · constructed response

A water tank already contains 12 liters. Water enters at 3 liters per minute. Write an equation for the amount $A$ after $m$ minutes and create a 3-row table that fits it.

Answer / solution

$A=3m+12$. One table is $(m,A)=(0,12),(2,18),(5,27)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a table, graph, equation, or context from another representation of the same relationship.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Representation: Context + Equation + Table

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · DOK 2 · Bloom Apply

Example 5

U1-S11-EX-005 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-MISMATCH · EX · Table + Equation · constructed response
xtable y
-16
04
12
21

A student says this table represents $y=-2x+4$. Find the row that does not fit and correct it.

Answer / solution

For $x=2$, the equation gives $y=0$, not 1. The corrected row is $(2,0)$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine whether two representations belong to the same relationship using evidence from values, rates, or patterns.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Representation: Table + Equation

Example 6

U1-S11-EX-006 · Notes Example · Section 1.1 · Family S11-EQUIV-EVIDENCE · EX · Equation + Ordered pairs · constructed response

Relationship A is $y=\frac12x-3$. Relationship B contains the points $(0,-3)$ and $(6,0)$. Determine whether they can be the same relationship and cite two pieces of evidence.

Answer / solution

Yes. Both contain $(0,-3)$, and the rate from $(0,-3)$ to $(6,0)$ is $\frac{3}{6}=\frac12$, matching the equation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine whether two representations belong to the same relationship using evidence from values, rates, or patterns.

Evidence/job: Model a canonical representation connection.

Representation: Equation + Ordered pairs

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: COMPARE-COMPETING-METHODS-01 · DOK 2 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

You Try It 4

U1-S11-YTI-004 · Notes YTI · Section 1.1 · Family S11-CONTEXT-TO-REP · YTI · Context + Equation + Table · constructed response

A bike rental starts with a 6-dollar fee and costs 4 dollars per hour. Write an equation for cost $C$ after $h$ hours and make a table for $h=0,2,5$.

Answer / solution

$C=4h+6$ with values $(0,6),(2,14),(5,26)$.

xtable y

29

A student says the table matches $y=3x+2$. Identify and correct any mismatch.

Answer / solution

At $x=2$ the equation gives 8, not 9. Replace $(2,9)$ with $(2,8)$.

You Try It 6

U1-S11-YTI-006 · Notes YTI · Section 1.1 · Family S11-EQUIV-EVIDENCE · YTI · Equation + Ordered pairs · constructed response

Relationship A is $y=-3x+2$. Relationship B contains $(0,2)$ and $(2,-4)$. Determine whether they are the same relationship and explain with values or rate.

Answer / solution

Yes. Both have starting value 2, and B has rate $(-4-2)/(2-0)=-3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine whether two representations belong to the same relationship using evidence from values, rates, or patterns.

Evidence/job: Apply the just-modeled idea with new values.

Representation: Equation + Ordered pairs

Stage / MG: Intro

Question 9

U1-S11-P1-009 · Practice Set 1 · Section 1.1 · Family S11-CONTEXT-TO-REP · F1-C1 · Context + Equation · constructed response

A candle is 24 cm tall and burns 2 cm each hour. Let $y$ be the amount after $x$ hours. Model the situation with an equation and interpret the constant term in context.

Answer / solution

$y=-2x+24$. The starting value 24 is the amount when $x=0$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a table, graph, equation, or context from another representation of the same relationship.

Evidence/job: Translate a meaningful context into an equation.

Question 11

A candle is 24 cm tall and burns 2 cm each hour. Let $y$ be the amount after $x$ hours. Create an equation for the situation and identify the initial amount before any change occurs.

Answer / solution

Section 1.2: Inputs, Outputs, and Functions

Learning target: Students will interpret functions as rules that connect inputs and outputs without relying on formal domain and range language.

WTC

What's to Come

U1-S12-WTC-001 · WTC · Section 1.2 · Family S12-WTC-FUNCTION-RULE · WTC · Table + Rule · constructed response
InputOutput
-25
-12
0-1
1-4
2-7
  1. Describe what happens to the output when the input increases by 1.
  2. Predict the output for input 3.
  3. Write a rule that connects input to output.
  4. Explain whether this rule gives exactly one output for each listed input.
Answer / solution

The output decreases by 3 for each increase of 1. The next output is -10. A rule is $y=-3x-1$. Each input shown has exactly one output, so the displayed relationship behaves as a function.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Students will interpret functions as rules that connect inputs and outputs without relying on formal domain and range language.

Evidence/job: Preview input-output rule thinking and the one-output-per-input idea.

Representation: Table + Rule

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01 · DOK 2-3 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Example 5

U1-S12-EX-005 · Notes Example · Section 1.2 · Family S12-CONTEXT-IO · EX · Context + Equation · constructed response

At a copy shop, input is the number of pages and output is total printing cost. The shop charges 8 cents per page. Name the input and output quantities and write a rule using dollars.

Answer / solution

Input: pages $p$. Output: cost $C$. A rule is $C=0.08p$ dollars.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a rule that connects inputs to outputs in a table, graph, equation, or context.

Evidence/job: Model the function-rule job.

Representation: Context + Equation

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: REPTRANS-TRANSLATE-VERBAL-SYMBOLIC-01 · DOK 2 · Bloom Apply

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Example 6

U1-S12-EX-006 · Notes Example · Section 1.2 · Family S12-PAIRS-TO-RULE · EX · Ordered pairs + Equation · constructed response

The ordered pairs are $(-1,4),(0,1),(1,-2),(2,-5)$. Formulate a rule and use it to predict the output when the input is 5.

Answer / solution

The rule is $y=-3x+1$. At $x=5$, $y=-14$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a rule that connects inputs to outputs in a table, graph, equation, or context.

Evidence/job: Model the function-rule job.

Representation: Ordered pairs + Equation

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: REPTRANS-GRAPH-TABLE-EQUATION-01 · DOK 2 · Bloom Apply

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Section 1.3: Function Families

Learning target: Students will recognize common function families from graphs, tables, equations, and contexts.

WTC

What's to Come

U1-S13-WTC-001 · WTC · Section 1.3 · Family S13-WTC-FAMILIES · WTC · Graph panel · constructed response
  1. Describe one visible feature of each graph.
  2. Which graph appears to have a constant rate of change?
  3. Which graph has a single lowest point and two straight arms?
  4. Match A-D to linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute value, and state one piece of evidence for each match.
Answer / solution

A is linear (straight line/constant rate), B is quadratic (parabola with a vertex), C is exponential (curved growth with a multiplicative pattern), and D is absolute value (V-shape with two linear arms).

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Students will recognize common function families from graphs, tables, equations, and contexts.

Evidence/job: Preview family recognition from visual structure.

Representation: Graph panel

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01 · DOK 2-3 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Notes Example

Example 1

U1-S13-EX-001 · Notes Example · Section 1.3 · Family S13-EQ-CLASSIFY · EX · Equation · constructed response

Classify each equation by family: $y=3x-2$, $y=x^2+1$, $y=2^x$, and $y=|x|-4$. State the structural clue in each equation.

Answer / solution

Linear, quadratic, exponential, absolute value. Clues are first power of x, squared x, variable exponent, and absolute-value bars.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design examples of linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute value relationships using more than one representation.

Evidence/job: Model family recognition across representations.

Representation: Equation

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: CONDITION-CLASSIFY-CASE-01 · DOK 1-2 · Bloom Apply

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Example 3

U1-S13-EX-003 · Notes Example · Section 1.3 · Family S13-GRAPH-COMPARE · EX · Graph · constructed response

Compare the two graphs. Classify each family and name one feature that distinguishes them.

Answer / solution

One graph is quadratic and one is exponential. The quadratic has a turning point and symmetric parabola shape; the exponential grows multiplicatively and does not have a turning point in the displayed window.

Exponential, because the balance changes by a constant multiplicative factor (1.05) each year.

Answer / solution

The claim is incorrect. First differences are 3,5,7 and second differences are constant at 2, which is quadratic evidence. Ratios are not constant.

A graph is V-shaped. A student immediately calls it quadratic. What additional evidence should be checked, and which family is more likely?

Answer / solution

Check whether the graph has two straight arms or curved parabolic sides and inspect equation/table structure. Straight arms indicate absolute value.

Section 1.4: Expressions and Structure

Learning target: Students will interpret and rewrite algebraic expressions by identifying structure, meaning, and equivalent forms.

WTC

What's to Come

U1-S14-WTC-001 · WTC · Section 1.4 · Family S14-WTC-TILES · WTC · Algebra tiles + Expression · constructed response
  1. Write an expression for the tiles.
  2. How could you group like tiles without changing the value?
  3. Imagine two identical copies of the entire tile set. Write one expression with parentheses and one without.
  4. Explain why the two expressions from part 3 are equivalent.
Answer / solution

The pictured tiles represent $3x+1$. Two copies can be written $2(3x+1)$ or $6x+2$. The distributive property shows the expressions are equivalent.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Students will interpret and rewrite algebraic expressions by identifying structure, meaning, and equivalent forms.

Evidence/job: Preview structure, grouping, and equivalence through a shared model.

Representation: Algebra tiles + Expression

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01 · DOK 2-3 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

Notes Example

Example 1

U1-S14-EX-001 · Notes Example · Section 1.4 · Family S14-COMBINE-STRUCTURE · EX · Expression · constructed response

In $5x-3+2x+8$, identify the terms and coefficients, then rewrite the expression by combining like terms.

Answer / solution

Terms: $5x,-3,2x,8$. Coefficients of variable terms: 5 and 2. Equivalent form: $7x+5$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build equivalent expressions by using structure, grouping, and properties of operations.

Evidence/job: Model expression structure/equivalence.

Representation: Expression

U1-S14-EX-002 · Notes Example · Section 1.4 · Family S14-DISTRIBUTE · EX · Expression · constructed response

Rewrite $4(2x-3)+5$ without parentheses.

Answer / solution

$8x-12+5=8x-7$.

Example 3

U1-S14-EX-003 · Notes Example · Section 1.4 · Family S14-AREA-DISTRIBUTE · EX · Area model + Expression · constructed response

Use the area model to explain why a product form and a sum of partial areas can be equivalent.

Answer / solution

The total rectangle area equals the sum of its smaller rectangle areas. Symbolically, multiplying a factor across a sum gives the same total as adding the partial products.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build equivalent expressions by using structure, grouping, and properties of operations.

Evidence/job: Model expression structure/equivalence.

Representation: Area model + Expression

Example 4

U1-S14-EX-004 · Notes Example · Section 1.4 · Family S14-FACTOR · EX · Expression · constructed response

Factor the greatest common factor from $12x+18$ and explain what the factored form makes easier to see.

Answer / solution

$12x+18=6(2x+3)$. The factored form makes the common factor 6 visible.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rewriting strategies to make the meaning of an expression easier to work with.

Example 6

U1-S14-EX-006 · Notes Example · Section 1.4 · Family S14-CONTEXT-EXPR · EX · Context + Expression · constructed response

A rectangle has length $x+5$ and width 3. Write its perimeter in two equivalent forms and explain what each form emphasizes.

Answer / solution

$P=2(x+5)+2(3)$ and $P=2x+16$. The first form shows the side-length structure; the simplified form shows total variable and constant parts.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rewriting strategies to make the meaning of an expression easier to work with.

You Try It 6

U1-S14-YTI-006 · Notes YTI · Section 1.4 · Family S14-CONTEXT-EXPR · YTI · Context + Expression · constructed response

A gym charges an enrollment amount $e$ plus 12 dollars for each class. Write an expression for 5 classes in two equivalent forms if the enrollment is split equally across two family members.

Answer / solution

Answers depend on how the split is defined; one coherent setup is total $e+60$, and each person’s equal share is $\frac{e+60}{2}=\frac e2+30$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rewriting strategies to make the meaning of an expression easier to work with.

Evidence/job: Apply the modeled structure with new values.

Section 1.5: Equations as Models

Learning target: Students will write, solve, and interpret equations that model situations.

WTC

What's to Come

U1-S15-WTC-001 · WTC · Section 1.5 · Family S15-WTC-RENTAL · WTC · Context + Quantity table + Equation · constructed response
QuantityValue
Starting rental fee12 dollars
Hourly rate4 dollars/hour
Total cost32 dollars
  1. Let $h$ represent rental hours. Write an equation that connects the quantities.
  2. Before solving exactly, estimate whether $h$ should be closer to 2, 5, or 10 hours.
  3. Find $h$ using any reasoning you trust.
  4. Explain what the solution means in the situation and how you can check it.
Answer / solution

$12+4h=32$. The answer should be near 5 because five groups of 4 add 20 to the starting 12. Solving gives $h=5$. Check: $12+4(5)=32$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Students will write, solve, and interpret equations that model situations.

Evidence/job: Preview modeling, solving, interpretation, and checking in one coherent situation.

Representation: Context + Quantity table + Equation

Stage / MG: Intro

Structure: WTC-FRQ-PREVIEW-01 · DOK 2-3 · Bloom Analyze

Source basis: Original Unit 1 Assessment Plan + Algebra 1 Framework v6.9 curated enrichment + current Question Structure Toolkit; wording authored fresh.

A gym charges a 25-dollar joining fee plus 8 dollars per visit. A member has paid 73 dollars. Let $v$ be the number of visits. Construct an equation.

Answer / solution

$25+8v=73$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct an equation that models a situation using meaningful quantities and operations.

V5 Question 8

U1-SUM-V5-Q08 · Secure Summative V5 · Section Summative · Family SUM-ALT-MODEL · V5-ALT-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed response

A club has 18 dollars and earns 6 dollars for each item sold. It needs 72 dollars. Write and solve an equation for the number of items, then decide whether the result is reasonable.

Answer / solution

$18+6n=72$, so $n=9$ items. The result is reasonable because $18+54=72$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve one-variable equations with equivalent transformations and verify and interpret solutions.

V5 Question 10

A room safely holds no more than 30 people. There are already 12 people inside and groups of 3 will enter. Write and solve an inequality for the number of full groups $g$ that can still enter, and interpret the greatest whole-number value.

Answer / solution

$12+3g\le30$, so $g\le6$. At most 6 full groups can enter.
